# norm — tables (second trajectory seed `a1_s43`)

Facts only. Generated by `analyze.py`; every diet is read on identical held-out rows, and the matched row sets never see a diet.

**Objects.**  `V[l, d](s_t)` is the outcome-trained critic: a ridge readout of the frozen state predicting `o_l(t + d)`, the actor being RIGHT at level `l` at position `t + d`, so higher `V` = a better world and a drop in `V` is bad news. `V_pre = V[l, a+1](s_(t-1))` is the norm: what the critic expected about the query's outcome one token BEFORE the event. `R = V[l, a](s_t) - V[l, a+1](s_(t-1))` is the revision at the event, both terms predicting the same outcome. `Ro` is `R` recomputed from the unedited stream's states at the same positions — the same window with nothing done to it. `d = outcome - V_pre` is the outcome surprise at the query. `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)` is the same-prefix contrast: within a twin pair `s_(t-1)` is bit-identical, so `V_pre` cancels and `dR = V[l, 0](s_t^viol) - V[l, 0](s_t^twin)`.

**Anchors.**  `fd` = the edit onset (`first_diff`), `tv` = the first exactly-impossible token. Violation rows are `etype == swap` with a `t_v`; at the `tv` anchor there are no unedited rows by construction, so the unedited reference there is `Ro` on the identical windows.

**Seed.** This file is the norm round re-run on a SECOND trajectory seed, `traj_a1_s43` (`seed 43`, `data_seed 8`), with the grammar held fixed (`rule_seed 0`, `alpha 1.0`, `weight_seed 1`) so the stimuli, the parse, the glitch world's legal law and the diets' `(etype, j)` cell histograms are the ones the banked round used and only the model moves. The banked seed's file is [`tables.md`](tables.md); section 0b below is the side-by-side of its headline rows against this one. The `striatum/` actor reproduction gate does not apply across seeds and was switched off with `--banked-actor none`.

Reproduction:

```bash
cd experiments   # MODAL_PROFILE=chromatic
D=/data/v16_s2_L6_m4_distinct/logit_reading
modal run --detach -m rhm.logit_reading.train_trajectory::train_trajectory \
    --tag a1_s43 --seed 43 --data-seed 8
modal run --detach -m rhm.logit_reading.coeruleus.readout::readout_ckpt \
    --ckpt $D/traj_a1_s43/step064000.pt --tag onnoise
for c in step008000:a1 step064000:a1 step064000:swap65k; do
  modal run -m rhm.logit_reading.striatum.addendum::hexcess_ckpt \
      --ckpt $D/traj_a1_s43/${c%%:*}.pt --stim-tag ${c##*:}
done
modal run --detach -m rhm.logit_reading.striatum.norm.task::norm_sweep \
    --cells "$D/traj_a1_s43/step000000.pt:a1:1,$D/traj_a1_s43/step008000.pt:a1:1,$D/traj_a1_s43/step064000.pt:a1:1,$D/traj_a1_s43/step000000.pt:swap65k:0,$D/traj_a1_s43/step008000.pt:swap65k:0,$D/traj_a1_s43/step064000.pt:swap65k:0"
modal run --detach -m rhm.logit_reading.striatum.norm.task::norm_sweep \
    --cells "$D/traj_a1_s43/step000000.pt:swap65k:0,$D/traj_a1_s43/step008000.pt:swap65k:0,$D/traj_a1_s43/step064000.pt:swap65k:0" --do-glitch --tag glitch
python -m rhm.logit_reading.striatum.norm.analyze <dir>/traj_a1_s43 \
    --outfile tables_s43.md --banked-actor none \
    --out rhm/logit_reading/striatum/norm/results \
    --figs rhm/logit_reading/striatum/norm/figs_s43
python -m rhm.logit_reading.striatum.norm.analyze <dir>/traj_a1_s43 \
    --mode addendum --addendum-tag glitch --tags swap65k --outfile tables_s43.md \
    --out rhm/logit_reading/striatum/norm/results \
    --figs rhm/logit_reading/striatum/norm/figs_s43
```

## 0b. Side-by-side against the banked seed `traj_a1_s42`

The banked round's headline rows, read off `tables.md` and this file at the identical cell. `s42` is the banked seed (`seed 42`, `data_seed 7`); `s43` is this one (`seed 43`, `data_seed 8`). The grammar is FIXED across the two (`rule_seed 0`, `alpha 1.0`, `weight_seed 1`), so the stimuli, the parse and the diets' cell histograms are shared and only the model moves. **Where the two disagree in sign the row is marked.**

**Does `dR` scale with the pre-event expectation?**  `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)`; the pair shares its prefix so `V_pre` is identical within it and the regression to the mean cancels.  The `R ~ V_pre` column is the confounded comparator.

**Token-identity guard.**  `phasic.py`'s `balance_tokens` subset, in which every token is the violator exactly as often as it is the twin. `dR` is a paired difference, so on such a subset any additive function of token identity cancels EXACTLY; the `signed token histogram` column is the self-check and must be 0.

At offsets > 0 both streams carry the SAME (edited) continuation, which is the swap's legally regrown subtree — coherent with the violator's token and not with the twin's — so the later offsets are not a pure decay of the event.

**Does `dR` scale with the pre-event expectation?**  `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)`; the pair shares its prefix so `V_pre` is identical within it and the regression to the mean cancels.  The `R ~ V_pre` column is the confounded comparator.

**Token-identity guard.**  `phasic.py`'s `balance_tokens` subset, in which every token is the violator exactly as often as it is the twin. `dR` is a paired difference, so on such a subset any additive function of token identity cancels EXACTLY; the `signed token histogram` column is the self-check and must be 0.

### 6. Q2 — the same-prefix twins (a1, step 64000)

| max |log q_(t-1) difference| (0 by construction)        | 0.00001049 |
| mean |ds| over the saved pairs                          | 0.7247     |
| mean |ds| at caliper 0.30                               | 0.1344     |

**Does `dR` scale with the pre-event expectation?**  `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)`; the pair shares its prefix so `V_pre` is identical within it and the regression to the mean cancels.  The `R ~ V_pre` column is the confounded comparator.

**Token-identity guard.**  `phasic.py`'s `balance_tokens` subset, in which every token is the violator exactly as often as it is the twin. `dR` is a paired difference, so on such a subset any additive function of token identity cancels EXACTLY; the `signed token histogram` column is the self-check and must be 0.

**Does `dR` scale with the pre-event expectation?**  `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)`; the pair shares its prefix so `V_pre` is identical within it and the regression to the mean cancels.  The `R ~ V_pre` column is the confounded comparator.

**Token-identity guard.**  `phasic.py`'s `balance_tokens` subset, in which every token is the violator exactly as often as it is the twin. `dR` is a paired difference, so on such a subset any additive function of token identity cancels EXACTLY; the `signed token histogram` column is the self-check and must be 0.

**Matched persistence after the event** (all pairs at caliper 0.3; the token-balanced version follows)

**`full`, level 1, offsets from `t_v` (n varies with the window end)**  Read offset 0 as the clean event reading: the pair differs only in the token at `t_v` there. **Does `dR` scale with the pre-event expectation?**  `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)`; the pair shares its prefix so `V_pre` is identical within it and the regression to the mean cancels.  The `R ~ V_pre` column is the confounded comparator.

**Token-identity guard.**  `phasic.py`'s `balance_tokens` subset, in which every token is the violator exactly as often as it is the twin. `dR` is a paired difference, so on such a subset any additive function of token identity cancels EXACTLY; the `signed token histogram` column is the self-check and must be 0.

### 6. Q2 — the same-prefix twins (swap65k, step 64000)

**swap65k, step 64000** — the same-prefix twin population (`phasic.py`'s construction: the violator replaced by the legal token of nearest model surprisal after an identical prefix).

| quantity                                                | value      |
|---------------------------------------------------------|------------|
| eligible violations                                     | 45000      |
| pairs saved (selection caliper 2.00)                    | 14843      |
| pairs at the parent's caliper 0.30                      | 4453       |
| pairs at 0.30, test split only                          | 1102       |
| max |V_pre(violator) - V_pre(twin)| (0 by construction) | 0.00000197 |
| max |log q_(t-1) difference| (0 by construction)        | 0.00001216 |
| mean |ds| over the saved pairs                          | 0.7513     |
| mean |ds| at caliper 0.30                               | 0.1373     |

**Does `dR` scale with the pre-event expectation?**  `dR = R(violator) - R(legal twin)`; the pair shares its prefix so `V_pre` is identical within it and the regression to the mean cancels.  The `R ~ V_pre` column is the confounded comparator.

**Token-identity guard.**  `phasic.py`'s `balance_tokens` subset, in which every token is the violator exactly as often as it is the twin. `dR` is a paired difference, so on such a subset any additive function of token identity cancels EXACTLY; the `signed token histogram` column is the self-check and must be 0.

# Addendum (2026-09-17) — the two worlds: where the continuation belongs to the flagged token, and where it does not

The main round found the outcome-trained critic pricing the illegal token as BETTER news than its surprisal-matched legal twin (`dR` > 0). On the swap stimuli the violator is the token the regrown continuation belongs to, so that sign is confounded with re-parsing being the right policy. This addendum adds `basalis/worlds.py`'s other world: the SAME window with no edit, the token at the SAME `t_v` replaced by an illegal one, where the continuation is the original stream and belongs to a legal token instead. Same twin construction (legal replacement at matched model surprisal after a bit-identical prefix, token-balanced, the parent's 0.30 caliper), same windows, same positions, same `(k*, j)` strata. `glitch_s`, not `glitch_m`: the glitch token is the illegal-under-the-clean-prefix token whose model surprisal is nearest the edit's own violator, since a uniform draw is 1.4-2.1 nats more surprising and would let the contrast win on the flagged token alone.

The clean-prefix legal law `p_L(. | Wo_<t)` is not on file for `swap65k` (`with_reference=False` in its build), so it was recomputed with `stimuli.py`'s own recipe and gated on the assertion that build makes — the unedited stream never hits probability zero (`norm_pLorig_swap65k.npz`).

## swap65k — the glitch world beside the edit world

### step 0

**swap65k, step 0** — the two worlds' pair populations.  Both are the same construction: the flagged token replaced by the LEGAL token of nearest model surprisal after a bit-identical prefix.  They differ in whose continuation follows: in the edit world the swap's regrown subtree, which belongs to the violator; in the glitch world the untouched original stream, which belongs to a legal token.

| world       | pairs saved | pairs at caliper 0.30 | max |dV_pre| | max |d log q_(t-1)| | mean |ds| in pair | mean surprisal, flagged token | mean surprisal, legal twin |
|-------------|-------------|-----------------------|--------------|---------------------|-------------------|-------------------------------|----------------------------|
| edit (swap) | 45000       | 36067                 | 0.00000000   | 0.00000000          | 0.0997            | 2.782                         | 2.786                      |
| glitch      | 45000       | 42042                 | 0.00000000   | 0.00000000          | 0.0643            | 2.787                         | 2.787                      |

The glitch's own guards (`glitch_s`: the illegal-under-the-clean-prefix token nearest the edit's violator in model surprisal):

| quantity                                                      | value                 |
|---------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|
| illegal token available under the clean prefix                | 1.0000                |
| legal tokens at t_v under the clean prefix (mean of 16)       | 5.173                 |
| mean |s(glitch) - s(edit violator)|  (the across-world guard) | 0.0491                |
| mean surprisal: glitch / edit violator / the original token   | 2.787 / 2.782 / 2.782 |
| P(the glitch token is the edit's violator)                    | 0.2497                |


**`dR` in the two worlds** — token-balanced, on the 35186 windows that survive BOTH worlds' caliper, so the strata are identical. `dR = R(flagged token) - R(legal twin)`; `V` predicts the actor being RIGHT, so `dR` > 0 means the flagged token is priced as the better news.

### step 8000

**swap65k, step 8000** — the two worlds' pair populations.  Both are the same construction: the flagged token replaced by the LEGAL token of nearest model surprisal after a bit-identical prefix.  They differ in whose continuation follows: in the edit world the swap's regrown subtree, which belongs to the violator; in the glitch world the untouched original stream, which belongs to a legal token.

| world       | pairs saved | pairs at caliper 0.30 | max |dV_pre| | max |d log q_(t-1)| | mean |ds| in pair | mean surprisal, flagged token | mean surprisal, legal twin |
|-------------|-------------|-----------------------|--------------|---------------------|-------------------|-------------------------------|----------------------------|
| edit (swap) | 21679       | 7605                  | 0.00000083   | 0.00000858          | 0.1325            | 2.748                         | 2.747                      |
| glitch      | 45000       | 5671                  | 0.00000000   | 0.00000000          | 0.1346            | 5.200                         | 5.182                      |

The glitch's own guards (`glitch_s`: the illegal-under-the-clean-prefix token nearest the edit's violator in model surprisal):

| quantity                                                      | value                 |
|---------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|
| illegal token available under the clean prefix                | 1.0000                |
| legal tokens at t_v under the clean prefix (mean of 16)       | 5.173                 |
| mean |s(glitch) - s(edit violator)|  (the across-world guard) | 2.6748                |
| mean surprisal: glitch / edit violator / the original token   | 7.446 / 4.870 / 1.016 |
| P(the glitch token is the edit's violator)                    | 0.2237                |


**`dR` in the two worlds** — token-balanced, on the 2453 windows that survive BOTH worlds' caliper, so the strata are identical. `dR = R(flagged token) - R(legal twin)`; `V` predicts the actor being RIGHT, so `dR` > 0 means the flagged token is priced as the better news.

### step 64000

**swap65k, step 64000** — the two worlds' pair populations.  Both are the same construction: the flagged token replaced by the LEGAL token of nearest model surprisal after a bit-identical prefix.  They differ in whose continuation follows: in the edit world the swap's regrown subtree, which belongs to the violator; in the glitch world the untouched original stream, which belongs to a legal token.

| world       | pairs saved | pairs at caliper 0.30 | max |dV_pre| | max |d log q_(t-1)| | mean |ds| in pair | mean surprisal, flagged token | mean surprisal, legal twin |
|-------------|-------------|-----------------------|--------------|---------------------|-------------------|-------------------------------|----------------------------|
| edit (swap) | 14843       | 4453                  | 0.00000197   | 0.00001216          | 0.1373            | 3.543                         | 3.537                      |
| glitch      | 45000       | 2608                  | 0.00000000   | 0.00000000          | 0.1381            | 7.703                         | 7.684                      |

The glitch's own guards (`glitch_s`: the illegal-under-the-clean-prefix token nearest the edit's violator in model surprisal):

| quantity                                                      | value                 |
|---------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|
| illegal token available under the clean prefix                | 1.0000                |
| legal tokens at t_v under the clean prefix (mean of 16)       | 5.173                 |
| mean |s(glitch) - s(edit violator)|  (the across-world guard) | 2.6788                |
| mean surprisal: glitch / edit violator / the original token   | 9.557 / 6.993 / 0.907 |
| P(the glitch token is the edit's violator)                    | 0.2246                |


**`dR` in the two worlds** — token-balanced, on the 869 windows that survive BOTH worlds' caliper, so the strata are identical. `dR = R(flagged token) - R(legal twin)`; `V` predicts the actor being RIGHT, so `dR` > 0 means the flagged token is priced as the better news.
